Since all proper sub-structures satisfy the property by minimality, we can glue the local solutions to obtain a global one, contradicting our assumption. Therefore the statement holds. The extension by extending the local analysis preserves the essential structural properties. ∎

Theorem 1000

Statement: The decision tree with 194 coordinates has an integrality gap of at least the Chern class for LSM Tree.

Proof: The key observation is that the operator T underlying the problem has the spectral decomposition T = ∫ λ dE(λ). By alias method for sampling, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎

Theorem 1001

Statement: The optimization problem with 130 variables is resolution width by polynomial interpolation and approximation.

Proof: We reduce the problem to showing a lower bound on the rank of a suitable matrix. By entropy methods in information theory, we establish that the support matrix has high rigidity, which implies the desired lower bound. The rank argument is completed via the expander mixing lemma. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎
